# AI Providers and Models

> **Navigation:** See [`project-map.md`](../project-map.md) for repository boundaries and Docker validation.

## Overview

Providers normalize chat, streaming, reasoning, tool calls, images, discovery, retries, and usage across built-in and extension-defined backends. Core owns provider execution, network policy, credential lifecycle/storage contracts, redaction, and non-secret published model metadata. Server composes those contracts for catalogue merging, custom providers/models, OAuth, and usage persistence; CLI uses the same Core services directly.

Provider responses become persisted messages through the conversation paths described in [`sessions.md`](./sessions.md); do not add a direct call path that bypasses hooks, cancellation, or persistence.

The [subscription-provider contract](./subscription-providers.md) documents the bounded GitHub Copilot implementation: explicit standalone CLI and guarded Server device login, issuer-isolated encrypted refresh, account-gated A/C/R routing and lifetime-bound native Server composition. It is not an API-key factory registration. Settings has separate operation-owned EN/FR static-Web fixture journeys. Interactive CLI has explicit issuer login and retired runtime lifetimes; emitted and installed regular/fullscreen/print/JSON/RPC fixtures retain their distinct scopes. The separate ChatGPT/Codex integration now includes its own OAuth/PKCE, account catalogue, CX transport/private replay, scoped strict/grammar tools and explicit WebSocket continuation. Full owner/profile/account qualification is not implied.

**MOD-03 closure:** the [final eight-axis audit](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-03-closure.json) verifies reasoning, private replay and request budgets through product `eda7593`, including the Anthropic zero-fallback reserve and Copilot cap/effort corrections. Model-owned image preparation is now delivered under the [scoped MOD-01.04b audit](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-image-preparation-closure.json), following explicit delegation; unknown-media/heuristic estimates are not billed usage. Wider MOD-01 delivery, manual real-account verification and QUA qualification remain open. Historical subset limitations below retain their source scope, not automatic current MOD-03 blockers.

**Current model-policy disposition:** the [six-family/eight-axis source-contract audit](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-effective-policy-closure.json), baseline `991c967`, marks MOD-01.01b/04a functionally DELIVERED. Actual Core defaults/sampling/tool/capability/context/media/continuation/response policies and native host evidence—not catalogue presence—support that result. Automatic flag/response effects were traced and fixed; optional foreign raw-sampling/hosted/named-section ABI is explicit and not silently promised. Dated PARTIAL/source-local caveats below retain historical scope, not current backlog. Cloud methods02e/final qualification06c and parent MOD-01 remain open; no new runtime/QUA/global credit from this documentary review.

## Architecture

```text
Core ProviderFactory -> AiProvider -> built-in or extension provider
Core model metadata -> shared published capabilities, limits and prices
Server registry -> credentials/custom dialect -> Core provider instance
Server ModelCatalog -> Core published + discovered + custom model entries
CLI/Web runtime -> streamChat -> normalized response and usage
```

## Public Contracts

- `AiProvider.chat()` returns one normalized `ChatResponse`.
- `AiProvider.streamChat()` emits incremental text/reasoning/tool events, awaits each `StreamChunkCallback`, and completes once. Built-ins provide producer backpressure; the shared turn rejects extension providers that overrun its bounded pending-event/byte queue instead of awaiting callbacks.
- `AiProvider.getAvailableModels()` returns bounded discovered identifiers when supported.
- `AiProvider.canDiscoverModels()` reports discovery eligibility, including explicit keyless HTTP(S) bases; it is not a connection/authentication probe.
- `AiProvider.getSpecializedModels()`, `classify()`, `classifyQuestions()` and `generateImages()` supply virtual/native capabilities through the bounded `SpecializedModels` SDK. Current-provider-only guest integration, supported native image models and exact ceilings are documented in [Code mode](./codemode.md). `before:model` (not conversational `before:provider`) gates hosted calls; the outer tool owns persistence/accounting. Remote image URLs are not fetched and provider credentials never enter guest descriptors/options.
- RPC now loads explicitly requested/trust-eligible extension generations before resolving custom provider/model selection, matching print/JSON. Even invalid custom selection may initialize those extensions; their stdout is still diverted to stderr, and no prompt is executed.
- `ChatOptions.signal` carries cancellation; `sessionId` supplies a bounded transport-continuation identity without becoming a credential; tools, thinking, model and retry options remain optional.
- `ProviderFactory` creates OpenAI/Azure, Anthropic, Gemini/Vertex, Bedrock, local OpenAI-compatible, mock, and registered extension providers.
- `ModelCatalogEntry` distinguishes `published`, `discovered`, `configured`, `custom`, and `virtual` sources with availability, capabilities, compatibility, context/output limits, and optional cache-aware pricing.

Published non-secret metadata lives in Core so CLI and Web use the same descriptors. Explicit thinking maps define supported levels (`null` means unsupported), while unknown models use conservative capabilities and remain unpriced. RPC caches and deduplicates live model discovery briefly, and can resolve CLI model-scope globs for cycling. Server alone merges live/custom catalogue state, but both composition roots use `ProviderCredentialService`. Raw keys/tokens stay in process memory or `EncryptedCredentialStore`; custom API keys use `provider:<private identity>` and headers use `provider-headers:<private identity>`. First-use identities retain the existing ID namespace; rename preserves that private identity, and old-ID reuse receives a fresh identity when it is reserved by a renamed record. Descriptors omit it. Catalogue, descriptor, RPC and HTTP objects are non-secret.

CLI and RPC layer explicit flags over agent settings `defaultProvider`, `defaultModel`, `enabledModels`, `defaultThinkingLevel`, and exact `modelThinkingLevels["provider/model"]`. A saved default inside the enabled scope is preferred; scope suffixes and explicit flags remain stronger, and the final level is capability-clamped. Agent-level retry settings configure shared turn retries. Core applies `transport`, `httpProxy`, `httpIdleTimeoutMs`, `websocketConnectTimeoutMs`, and bounded `retry.provider` timeouts/retries to built-in and extension/custom provider adapters. Network errors are stable and never include the request URL, remote body/headers, or proxy credentials.

Credential resolution order is environment, trusted `providerApiKeyCommands`, process memory, then encrypted storage. Command output is bounded, cached only in the process, and immediately registered with the shared redactor. OAuth endpoints and responses are bounded and HTTPS-only; refreshed access/refresh tokens replace the encrypted record without crossing descriptor boundaries. For admitted registrations, the redactor covers raw, URI, base64/base64url, hex and JSON-escaped variants, recursively bounds JSON traversal, and supplies independent stateful streams for chunked channels. Registration itself is bounded; see [credential publication and redaction](./native-security-contract.md#credential-lifecycle-and-export-scope).

The [05b source/host review](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-offline-host-review.json) binds current offline startup/reload/selection/failure contracts without a universal network ban. Server cold admission now releases a failed promise for a later authorized request and passes the host lifetime to generic built-in credential resolution; first failure stays visible, store stays unchanged and shutdown refuses late refresh publication. A later request may re-run existing trusted bootstrap, including configured commands; no internal/background retry or global Server offline switch. `packages/server/src/api/provider-credential-recovery-http.test.ts` adds two real HTTP cases;44 Docker cases/two plain-Node emitted probes/one strict root PASS with existing CLI29/Core6/current-source/Settings receipts reused.05b delivered; future cloud sources retain their source-specific gate obligations, not a model-host campaign or MOD-03 redelivery.

The [native Anthropic fallback review](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-anthropic-fallback-review.json) is explicitly corrected: its all17-zero nomination conclusion was erroneous (the unchanged local diagnostic already listed Fable5). Only `claude-fable-5` nominates ordered `claude-opus-4-8`, then `claude-opus-5`, each with published costs. Do not infer other targets/beta/alias pricing from an optional codec. `anthropic-fallback.test.ts` adds one compiled public-Core negative case/four JSON-SSE requests on Haiku5.5/Sonnet4.6; five existing accounting/catalogue controls reused. A speculative fallback patch was rejected and removed despite four synthetic green cases; it is not delivered policy or an accepted gap. That historical negative proof made no product/default/replay change;01b/04a remain PARTIAL.

The [separate native Fable5 delivery](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-anthropic-fable-fallback.json), baseline `27aa57d`, implements that one nominated default for original constructors: ordered `fallbacks` plus `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`. Declared forward prefix markers are omitted from model context; undeclared/reversed/mid-output/incomplete transitions fail before pending tools/completion. Requested logical model/API/replay identity stays Fable; exact target headers or reconciled per-model `usage.iterations` determine price only. Unknown/inconsistent attribution keeps physical counters but no invented cost; an internal refusal marker stops explicit `CacheWarmer` tariff fallback, without enabling any automatic warming. Derived/mutable metadata, forged `ownsNativeProfiles`/`ownsReplay` fields and arbitrary aliases confer no native policy; this owner's construction intent is now an ECMAScript-private captured flag, not a writable TS-private property. Six new compiled-public-Core cases plus45 reused accounting/cache/live-OAuth/native-HTTP controls:51 Docker runtime/one strict root/four equal product witnesses. No new host field, issuer authority, dependency, session format, complete beta lifecycle or account/service certificate;01b/04a still PARTIAL.

### Native construction intent boundaries

The [Google/Vertex/Bedrock intent receipt](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-native-owner-intent.json), baseline `b7a1af9`, fixes three demonstrated private-policy promotions. `GeminiProvider.nativeScope` and `BedrockProvider.ownsNativeProfiles` were TypeScript-private own properties, not immutable runtime authority. Their construction values and reads now use readonly ECMAScript-private fields in `packages/core/src/providers/index.ts`: genuine instances cannot be downgraded by former-name getters, and derived transports cannot acquire native schema/header/price/routing behavior through them. This changes no model defaults, credential source/priority/exchange/cache, public option, SDK/host field or dependency. It is not a general sandbox against privileged Node code.

`packages/core/src/providers/native-owner-intent.test.ts` adds three compiled public-Core cases: Google/Vertex JSON-SSE schema/auth preservation and zero forged getter evaluation in both directions; derived Bedrock unsupported strict/choice refuses before transport, while genuine JSON/binary requests keep native controls despite a downgrade getter. Three predecessor REDs retained; first candidate build/types/one strict root/85 runtime (three new+82 reused)/one equal product witness PASS,832 unselected. Existing private defaults/signed replay/cancellation/static profile/Bearer-SigV4/Vertex key controls remain source-specific. No new account/CLI/API/browser/installed/production campaign, auth-file execution, automatic warming, accepted incident or MOD-03 claim;01b/04a remain PARTIAL pending remaining owner/default reconciliation.

The [Azure follow-up](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-azure-owner-intent.json), baseline `6d8da97`, likewise makes direct C prepared-default/catalogue intent and mixed-facade catalogue intent truly private. It does not change the separate `nativeAzureCompletions` delegate/runtime grants. Two added cases in the same compiled-public-Core test cover direct C JSON/SSE caps/deployment/catalogue and mixed45-model publication, with zero old-name getter evaluation for genuine/derived consumers. Two genuine predecessor REDs (after correcting one missing synthetic endpoint) precede first build/types/one strict root/21 runtime (two new+19 reused)/one equal product witness PASS;68 Azure cases unselected. No new caller/host/auth/default value or account/global/installed/browser campaign. Remaining01b/04a work is six-family default/sampling/capability source-contract reconciliation, not these delivered field guards.

### Original Anthropic historical tool loadouts

The [history receipt](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-anthropic-tool-history.json), baseline `c12bef8`, connects existing private v2 loadouts to the same seven eligible native models. `packages/core/src/agent/provider-turn.ts` captures active ancestry after hooks/current loadout publication; `packages/core/src/providers/anthropic-tool-history.ts` binds copied data privately to that owned request/first-party physical owner/model, including the live OAuth delegate. No new public option or [session format](./sessions.md): definitions are historical request data, never authority to register or execute tools. Current `systemPrompt` semantics are unchanged; old private prompt snapshots are not revived as current instructions.

Nonempty initial tools remain fixed with the existing placeholder/beta. Later removals use name references; additions and same-name replacements carry converted definitions by value before the next visible assistant/end, never between tool use/results. Initial-empty conversations keep current flat tools without gaining deferred mode later; removing all current tools preserves initial native eligibility. Scope changes reset history, compaction uses its checkpoint, and superseded attempts before the same visible assistant collapse to their final loadout. Historical schemas count conservatively against owned context admission, not ignored private metadata. The additional history read reuses cancellable admission; stale session/leaf context refuses without retrying on a new branch. Existing strict conversion/OAuth canonical names, signed replay/managed effort/cache placement/current executable registry remain authoritative.

`packages/core/src/providers/anthropic-tool-history.test.ts` supplies eleven compiled public-Core cases for post-hook/cold restart, initial-empty/all-removed/re-addition, raw/live OAuth signed tool adjacency, provider/model/branch/compaction, budget, superseded attempts and held-read withdrawal. `packages/cli/src/cli/anthropic-tool-history-process.test.ts` adds one real builtin JSON CLI journey over verified TLS: three processes, last offline, old schemas cannot restore a removed executable while the new tool executes, delta cache/current journal/privacy0600 and six physical requests billed once (60 tokens/$0.00054).163 distinct Docker cases (12 new+151 reused), two strict roots/build/types/four equal production witnesses PASS;218 profile cases unselected. No fresh browser/API/installed campaign is credited.

### Original Anthropic caller-tool declarations

The dated subset captures below retained deferred declarations as then-unbound evidence; the separate initial-scaffold delivery above now qualifies those full arrays without retroactively changing their results or implying full tool-loadout history.

Direct `AnthropicProvider` ownership and an exact approved effective model admit `applyAnthropicProfileToolDeclarations` in `providers/anthropic-profiles.ts`. Caller tools retain name/description, add `eager_input_streaming:true`, omit `strict` and project the schema root to `{type:'object',properties,required}`, defaulting absent properties/required to empty object/array. Root title/description/additionalProperties are not forwarded. No tools means no declaration array or tool choice. ### Native Bedrock binary streaming

Built-in `BedrockProvider.streamChat` posts explicitly Bearer- or SigV4-authenticated JSON to `/model/{encoded-model}/converse-stream` and consumes AWS binary EventStream, not a buffered JSON Converse facade. A configured HTTP(S) runtime/proxy root or full native Converse/ConverseStream endpoint preserves its prefix; explicit per-turn models replace the encoded model segment. URL credentials/query/fragment refuse on this streaming path. `chat()` retains the existing JSON Converse operation. The adapter's conversational API binding is `bedrock-converse-stream`; successful streaming responses persist this inert API through the existing shared message path, independently of an extension's operator-selected ID.

The internal decoder verifies prelude/message CRC32, UTF-8 JSON and bounded headers before dispatch. Limits remain 1 MiB/frame and tool input, 16 MiB aggregate payload, 64 MiB transport, 100,000 events and 1,024 blocks. Arbitrary network fragmentation/coalescing is supported without unbounded frame buffering. Ordered text/reasoning callbacks are awaited; cancellation interrupts held reads and forbids later buffered callbacks/completion. Complete tool arguments publish only after a valid stopped message and EOF; malformed arguments, exceptions/refusals, truncation, bad checksums and invalid block ordering cannot publish tools or success. Diagnostics exclude remote exception bodies.

Legacy/unknown streams admit safe-integer input/output usage; genuine cache counters survive, and a missing total derives from input plus output. Because the existing chat usage contract requires both primary counters, incomplete/invalid pairs omit usage rather than inventing a missing zero. Unknown pricing remains unknown. SigV4 double-escapes the encoded HTTP model path; keys/session tokens use actual HTTP-header validation. An independent fixed Python signing vector and loopback wire controls are local evidence, not AWS account qualification.

Native cache/opaque scopes use a tagged gateway/grant identity distinct from API-key hashes, retaining inference endpoint/model/provider/header/exact-constructor fences. Token rotation within one grant preserves signed continuation and known-empty; a new login, API key or derived constructor cannot borrow it. Existing API-key hashes and behavior remain compatible.

### Native Radius OAuth CLI

The [CLI issuer receipt](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-radius-oauth-cli.json) adds `auth login radius --issuer radius` and `/login radius --issuer radius`, with device default or `--method browser`. `RADIUS_GATEWAY` selects the issuer (Core default when absent); the stored gateway remains authoritative on restart without that environment hint. Environment/key/command precedence remains; a selected offline command cannot fall through to stored OAuth. The resolver clips per-operation refresh to runtime policy and borrows runtime/caller cancellation. ### Native TypeSafe classifier SDK subset

Public `TypesafeProvider` implements JSON `typesafe-system-one` at `https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone`. Explicit HTTP(S) bases append `/systemone`; full endpoints and proxy paths survive. URL credentials/query/fragment refuse. The caller supplies the Bearer API key; the constructor neither loads credentials nor starts authentication. Unrepresentable keys and header-construction failures refuse without echoing private strings. It reuses shared bounded JSON requests/responses and network policy; the `SpecializedModels` bridge additionally owns its existing ten-second hook/call deadline.

```js
import { TypesafeProvider, SpecializedModels } from '@ai-harness/core';
const models = new SpecializedModels(new TypesafeProvider({
  type: 'typesafe', apiKey: process.env.TYPESAFE_API_KEY,
}));
const result = await models.classifyQuestions({model: 'jev-latest',
  state: {message: 'Approved'},
  questions: {approved: {type: 'boolean', prompt: 'Does the message approve?'}}});
```

`TYPESAFE_CLASSIFIER_MODEL` and bounded SDK/guest descriptors preserve the one pinned Jev classifier's name, API, 64,000 context window and zero published token/cache prices. These are reviewed static data, not current free-service, availability or automatic context-reserve guarantees. Operator-selected provider IDs do not change the native binding. An explicitly configured unknown model gets no speculative name/window/price. This provider advertises no chat/discovery/image model; unsupported chat fails locally, never through a chat-completions fallback.

Labels and finite choices consume complete native choice distributions. Booleans map to `noul`, with the exact binary complement and a `>= 0.5` selected value. Finite numeric scores are requested as choices and restored to numbers: native scalar-score confidence is never turned into a fabricated distribution. Nonobject JSON state is wrapped in `{input: state}`. Missing/foreign/malformed answers fail; absent/invalid usage counters remain unknown, and zero calculated token cost requires both genuine counters on the pinned profile. Partial usage is not a complete billing certificate. Decoded JSON usage is admitted before answers: malformed/missing answers throw public `SpecializedModelError` with an owned frozen numeric `usage` copy. The bounded SDK preserves that accounting with a fixed diagnostic, and code mode records it once on the failed nested call/outer result, even when the guest catches the failure. Ordinary errors with an arbitrary `usage` property are not promoted. Unknown counters/prices, HTTP failures without decoded accounting, unparseable bodies and calls arriving after sandbox settlement do not invent usage. This exception contract is not Pi's complete stop-reason/error-result ABI.

Direct `TypesafeProvider.classifyNativeQuestions(request, {signal?})` separately exposes public `NativeClassifierRequest`, `NativeClassifierQuestion`, `NativeClassifierAnswer` and `NativeClassifierResult`. Use object `state` and named questions with `instructions`: `bool` criteria describe `true`/`false`, `choice` criteria map labels to descriptions, and `score` criteria are descriptive strings. Bool sends `noul` and returns `{type:'bool',probability}`; scalar answers retain finite `score` and independent [0,1] `confidence`, never an invented histogram. Choice answers require every probability, valid membership and a unit sum. Only the configured model is available; nonempty images refuse before dispatch. Owned inert JSON is capped at 32 KiB, 16 questions and 100 criteria/question, with 500-byte IDs/labels and 4,096-byte instructions/descriptions. Getters/cycles/unsafe JSON refuse without evaluation.

The direct result contains API/provider/model, complete `answers`, timestamp, optional observed `usage` and `stopReason: 'stop' | 'error' | 'aborted'`. Failures clear all answers and return a fixed `errorMessage`, not remote bodies/causes. Already decoded accounting survives invalid answers or observed withdrawal; HTTP/unparseable/held-body failures do not invent counters. Unknown prices remain unknown. This direct method reuses provider network policy but does **not** enter `SpecializedModels` hooks, its ten-second deadline, guest publication or automatic journal accounting. It is a native SDK contract, not Pi wire/result ABI compatibility.

Standalone `runCodemode` retains the finite-domain contract above; it does not expose this scalar method. The original SDK slice alone adds no host/account qualification; the later additive host delivery below remains separate. See [the classifier input map](./codemode.md#native-classifier-questions-and-providerinput-map) and [the remaining matrix](./provider-matrix.md).

### Native mixed Cloudflare Workers AI SDK subset

Public `CloudflareWorkersAiMixedProvider` explicitly delegates the existing eighteen-profile C chat SDK and three-profile native S classifier SDK with one `CloudflareWorkersAiConfig`. It does not repurpose the existing classifier-only `CloudflareWorkersAiProvider` or reserve `cloudflare-workers-ai` in `ProviderFactory`. Supply an explicit account/key or safe AI/proxy root; version/full C or full S endpoints normalize operation-owned paths. Default account endpoints remain `/ai/v1/chat/completions` and `/ai/run`; URL credentials/query/fragment refuse privately. An explicitly supplied S `/v2/run` proxy path is preserved, while its C counterpart uses `/v2/chat/completions`.

Only reviewed C IDs can configure or execute conversations; native S/unknown chat IDs refuse before mutation/I/O. Static listing exposes eighteen chats, discovery false. `getModelApi(id)` binds reviewed C versus S independently of operator aliases, unknown undefined. The specialized view contains the configured C virtual classifier plus all three native S classifiers (four total), no image generator or automatic DALL-E fallback. Native scalar questions remain on the separate S SDK, not a new guest ABI.

C labels/questions retain virtual JSON and physical disjoint cache pricing; S uses native `{model,input:{state,questions}}`, Cloudflare envelopes, its own windows/prices and decoded failed-answer usage. One guest can call both without changing hooks, deadlines, budgets or success-only store. A failed S continuation bills once and discards failed script state. Each operation constructs/captures its child before awaiting; an awaited C callback may dispatch S and change the configured C model without changing pending wire/API/provider/model/accounting. Both use explicit Bearer, never Azure/Anthropic credentials selected by logical names.

Owning and genuine outside-checkout installed consumers exercise this composition; the independent raw-data comparator separately checks all eighteen C/three S profiles, seven endpoint forms, four aliases, native/virtual guest boundaries and awaited withdrawal. These foundation SDK checks do not qualify automatic CLI/HTTP/Settings composition,gateway credentials/placeholders,stored/environment auth-host merging,live accounts,resize/default/reserve/full profiles or platform acceptance. The later independently acquired private C subset above owns only its named direct/mixed carrier scopes,not native S or complete host qualification. See [the matrix](./provider-matrix.md).

Profiles retain names, contexts, output maxima, prices, effort maps and declared compatibility/input limits. The declared 512-MiB/1,500-image/resize data is not an effective request allowance or installed resizer; existing shared/guest limits remain authoritative. Grammar/additional/search tools, encrypted reasoning replay, complete context/default/media policies and full effective profiles are not delivered by this metadata projection; the later [encrypted continuation subset](#original-responses-encrypted-continuation-subset) has its own bounded evidence.

R operations capture their child/model/provider before awaiting callbacks, require completed SSE, preserve native visible reasoning/tool handling and refuse further delivery after awaited withdrawal. Recognized value-free `ProviderContextOverflowError` authority remains typed for shared recovery; arbitrary remote failure prose remains private. Reviewed policy maps/clamps effort, uses developer input for reasoning profiles and system input otherwise, default non-strict function tools, minimum output 16, explicit prompt-cache modes versus legacy 24h retention, Unicode-safe 64-character keys and session affinity (suppressed for retention none). Existing Responses SSE/WebSocket/cached/fallback transport is reused without changing legacy/Azure policy. Genuine primary input disjoins observed cache reads/writes; strict tiers apply to gross input. Native R additionally requires a safe observed total: deficits/malformed totals omit usage, and surplus totals preserve counts without inferred cost. Unknown/inconsistent primary accounting stays unknown, not free. D finite/native scalar contracts and once-only decoded failed-answer billing remain delegated unchanged.

Focused Docker/installed evidence and retained initial operation/default-tool reds are recorded in [MOD-01](../../audit/pi/AUDIT-5.md). Historical E42 full-unit/parity/production failures remain distinct from focused SDK passes; its failed production runs no production probes. Shared offline catalogue, CLI startup/RPC current/list/select/cycle projections and Server catalogue/runtime capability/price lookups use that actual adapter declaration independently of operator names. Protocol default URLs require a matching physical API; an R adapter called `anthropic` does not acquire Anthropic's URL. Undeclared legacy bindings retain their compatibility projection. These standard URL defaults are not the adapter's actual endpoint provenance, even when a custom alias shares its API; full transport descriptors remain unqualified.

OpenRouter's 21 ambiguous zero-only profiles omit prices rather than declaring free calls; its bounded view copies no pricing tiers. ### Native OpenAI Decisions classifier SDK subset

Public `OpenAiDecisionsProvider` explicitly posts to `https://api.openai.com/v1/decisions`; HTTP(S) versioned/proxy roots or full endpoints preserve their prefixes/encoded paths. URL credentials/query/fragment refuse. Supply a Bearer API key directly; the adapter never loads ambient keys, foreign credentials or ChatGPT subscriptions. It is separate from `OpenAiProvider`/Responses, advertises no chat/discovery/image-generation model and reserves no factory identity.

Native requests contain `{model,input,questions}`. State becomes JSON text; validated images instead produce one user input containing `input_text` and PNG/JPEG/WebP data-URL `input_image` parts. Named bool questions become `predicate` with true/false meanings appended to instructions; choices become `choices:[{value,description?}]`; native scalar criteria become `levels:[{label}]`. Named array answers are normalized without accepting duplicates, foreign/missing answers, refusals or duplicate probability labels. Existing complete unit-sum probability and finite-score/confidence checks remain authoritative. Finite SDK/guest numeric domains still use choices, not scalar confidence as a fabricated histogram; nonobject finite state remains its original JSON value on this route.

`classifyNativeQuestions` uses the distinct direct native result contract above, including body-free errors and observed decoded usage on failed answers. `SpecializedModels`/standalone `runCodemode` retain finite labels/questions, cancellable hooks, the ten-second deadline and once-only failed-call accounting. Direct owned request JSON remains capped at 32 KiB including images; finite state/questions retain 32 KiB plus the existing at-most-four-image/8-MiB decoded image and guest JSON ceilings. Pi's 128-image endpoint allowance is not adopted. HTTP 504 is not retried on this route; other statuses retain the configured shared retry policy, and ordinary providers keep their existing 504 behavior. No remote failure prose is exposed.

Frozen `OPENAI_DECISIONS_MODEL` pins GPT-6 Luna (`gpt-6-luna`), text/image input, 922,000 context and base input $0.10 per million. Its readonly `pricingTiers` declares input $0.20 per million strictly above 272,000 input tokens, applied to all input tokens, not only the excess. Output/cache prices are zero; cost still requires genuine primary counters. Bounded SDK/guest descriptors expose base `pricing`, not a complete tier descriptor; the public constant and observed usage retain the tier policy. Unknown configured IDs remain unpriced and gain no speculative vision capability. These are reviewed static data, not current service/availability, context-reserve or complete profile qualification.

This is explicit native SDK/standalone finite-guest delivery only. The mixed R/D SDK above is a separate composition. Automatic multi-provider composition beyond the explicit custom host, ChatGPT/OAuth issuer restrictions and authorized vendor/platform qualification remain open in [the matrix](./provider-matrix.md).

### Explicit Anthropic workload SDK

The [WIF SDK receipt](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-anthropic-wif-sdk.json) qualifies explicit `AnthropicProviderConfig.anthropicFederation`/public `AnthropicFederationConfig`: rule/org/identity-token-file, optional service-account/workspace. No environment/profile discovery. Configuration and headers are captured; passive validation/metadata does not read the file or establish access, explicit offline discovery returns[] not a verified cache.

```typescript
const provider = new AnthropicProvider({
  type: 'anthropic', model: 'claude-sonnet-4-6',
  anthropicFederation: {
    federationRuleId: 'fdrl-example', organizationId: 'org-example',
    identityTokenFile: '/run/secrets/workload.jwt',
  },
});
```

The configured native base owns `/v1/oauth/token` and inference, consistently normalizes trailing slashes and accepts HTTPS or loopback only for credential exchange. Explicit projected symlinks work; nonblocking regular-file/16KiB assertion/fatal UTF8 bounds, shared256KiB response/actual Bearer Header validation, manual redirects,15s auth and shared caller/scope/deadline clipping prevent late publication/hostile-body waits. JSON JWT-bearer exchange sends rule/org/optional IDs and oauth+federation betas; short-lived token re-exchanges without a refresh token. Each instance/caller owns cache/publication; >120s cached,30–120 foreground advisory refresh can fall back outside the mandatory≤30s window, authenticated401 retries once after invalidation. No background work/dedup/global or disk token cache; the foreground advisory scheduling is deliberately simpler than the reference SDK background advisory task.

Inference sends Bearer/API OAuth beta but no Claude identity/system/canonical-tool policy or federation exchange beta. Existing native A profiles/reasoning/tools/accounting/raw signed history remain unchanged. Seven old-image REDs plus protected-header and trailing-base strengthened REDs; final build/types/two strict roots/34 runtime (seven new)/one compiled public SDK smoke PASS, three production inputs image-identical. JSON/signed history/SSE/symlink rotation/401/advisory/mandatory/cache isolation/cancellation/file-response bounds plus existing native grant/issuer/eight scoped API-key controls, not a new all-model matrix. Failed fixture/runtime/strict gates and exact overlays remain in receipt; old profiles strict fixture remains baseline/runtime-only. The [trusted CLI WIF fallback](#anthropic-workload-cli) now separately qualifies actual composition/privacy/retirement; [Server WIF](#anthropic-workload-server) is now also qualified separately; no native host/access/entitlement/installed/production/04b or MOD-03/historical repair claim.

### Anthropic workload CLI

The [CLI receipt](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-anthropic-wif-cli.json) admits complete trusted `ANTHROPIC_FEDERATION_RULE_ID`/`ANTHROPIC_ORGANIZATION_ID`/`ANTHROPIC_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE`, with optional `ANTHROPIC_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_ID`/`ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID`, only online after effective key/raw-token, configured-command and canonical stored/native-grant precedence. SDK now captures optional process-local signal/redactor references: file assertion/minted access token enroll in the same host redactor before exchange/inference; runtime close clips old instances/cache/hostile body without borrowing another caller. Built-in factory configuration, account filtering/OAuth, full defaults/media/cache/history profiles and authorized vendor/platform qualification remain open in [the matrix](./provider-matrix.md).

### Native OpenRouter Chat Completions SDK subset

Public `OpenRouterCompletionsProvider` explicitly implements C (`openai-completions`) at `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions`. HTTP(S) API/proxy roots append `/chat/completions`; full endpoints and encoded prefixes survive. URL credentials/query/fragment refuse. Actual HTTP headers admit an explicit Bearer key and optional configured headers with value-free failures; no ambient key, OAuth/login, reserved factory or automatic host configuration is added. Operator IDs such as `azure` or `local` do not change the endpoint/authentication, usage request or native API binding.

Frozen `OPENROUTER_COMPLETION_MODELS` projects the 377 pinned C identities/names/context/output limits, reasoning maps, input declarations, token tables and declared compatibility/resize data. Only these C IDs are admitted; unknown/other-API IDs refuse before I/O rather than guessing a transport. `getAvailableModels()` returns that static reviewed list, while `canDiscoverModels()` is false: no `/models/user` request, account filter or live availability claim. Metadata remains separate from host catalogue composition and complete effective profiles.

Requests use nested `reasoning.effort`, not generic `reasoning_effort`, with per-model off/level clamping. Exact direct/mixed C uses independently bound `store:false`,`max_completion_tokens` and ordinary `strict:false`;derived legacy policy remains separate. Physical roles/nine required-empty rows and typed private versus foreign visible history follow the [independent C subset](#native-openrouter-private-chat-completions-continuation-subset) above,not blanket unsigned reasoning replay. Eligible Anthropic-compatible C profiles add ephemeral instruction/last-user/tool cache markers. `sessionId` supplies bounded `x-session-id`; `cacheRetention:'none'` suppresses automatic affinity/cache markers. Numeric thinking budgets are not consumed. Known text-only profiles refuse images; the 257 declared image-input profiles use native text/data-URL parts, not image-output modalities. Declared resizing is not implemented.

JSON and inherited bounded SSE support visible reasoning/text and complete tools. Exact direct/mixed C retains independently bound private details/raw fields and requires native terminal authority;derived legacy still normalizes unsigned detail text/summary and refuses opaque encrypted/signature replay. Awaited callback cancellation forbids subsequent buffered text/tools/completion. JSON refusals/mixed errors, invalid JSON content and unsupported reasoning have body-free diagnostics. This is not a complete SSE grammar, signed transcript or per-profile transport certificate.

C `cached_tokens` is a read count; `cache_write_tokens` is separate. Observed legacy `prompt_cache_hit_tokens`/top-level `cached_tokens` also normalize. Do **not** subtract writes from C reads: for prompt 10/read 4/write 1, uncached input is 5. I's separate inclusive-cache contract still yields uncached 6/read 3/write 1 for the same numeric fields. `openrouter-usage.ts` selects this rule by actual API, never the shared endpoint/operator/model name. Invalid/incomplete primary pairs or impossible disjoint sums omit chat usage instead of inventing zeros.

The 356 nonzero token tables admit estimated cost from genuine primary counters; 21 ambiguous zero-only tables, including free/router IDs, remain unpriced. Seventy-three profiles retain 75 readonly tier thresholds, applied strictly above the observed gross input threshold to all tokens. Bounded virtual-classifier descriptors expose only base prices. This is static reviewed pricing, not a personalized invoice or service qualification.

The only specialized capability is the existing virtual classifier (`<chat-id>:classifier`), not native System One or DALL-E/image generation. Native I/T remain separate adapters. Four model IDs occur in both C and I: an ID or common `/chat/completions` URL alone cannot establish the operation/API. The mixed SDK/custom-host path above separately composes A/C/I/T. OAuth/account filtering, complete host descriptors/defaults/context reserves/media policies and general failed-chat accounting remain open. Ordinary chat/virtual failure behavior is not the decoded failed-answer billing certificate of native T/I. See [the remaining matrix](./provider-matrix.md).

### Native OpenRouter classifier SDK subset

Public `OpenRouterClassifiersProvider` posts native `{model,state,questions}` to `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/systemone`. Explicit HTTP(S) proxy/version/full endpoints preserve prefixes/encoded paths; URL credentials/query/fragment refuse. Keys and headers remain explicit, validated by actual HTTP representation, with static secret-free errors. This is `typesafe-system-one`, including for `openai/gpt-6-luna-decisions`, not OpenAI's separate Decisions endpoint, Cloudflare's `/run` envelope or a chat fallback. It adds no factory reservation, ambient authentication, OAuth, model discovery or automatic host configuration.

Frozen `OPENROUTER_CLASSIFIER_MODELS` projects all 21 installed classifier identities/names/APIs/context windows. Public readonly `declaredInput` retains six catalogue text/image declarations, but the pinned System One transport rejects **all** nonempty images. SDK/guest descriptors therefore never advertise `imageInput:true`; direct requests also refuse images before I/O. Do not equate catalogue declarations with an effective image contract.

`declaredPricing` preserves all reviewed input/output/cache rates. Eighteen nonzero token tables also supply SDK `pricing`; three zero-only fallback tables omit it and calculated USD cost, including IDs ending in `:free`. Upstream zero-fallback semantics do not establish a free invoice. Known cost requires genuine input/output counters; partial counters remain partial and unknown configured models remain unpriced. Declared cache rates are review data, not fabricated cache usage or a complete request profile. Physical OpenRouter Jev/Cloudflare/Luna windows/prices are not inherited from similarly named direct services.

The shared bounded classifier engine retains direct bool/choice/native-scalar results and the separate finite label/question SDK/standalone guest contract: `noul`, complete finite distributions, numeric domains lowered to choices and nonobject finite state wrapped in `{input:state}`. Decoded failed-answer usage survives typed errors and once-only failed guest accounting; failed scripts publish no store, while unpriced successful calls retain `costComplete:false`. Direct JSON, hooks/ten-second finite deadline, guest/output limits, cancellation and ordinary retries (including 504) remain unchanged. No direct scalar method is added to QuickJS.

Explicit `model` narrows the SDK catalogue; default construction exposes all 21 text-executable profiles. This separate SDK does not configure hosts; use the explicit mixed SDK/custom-host path above for A/C/I/T composition. Account-filtered catalogue, OAuth, complete effective profiles and live-service/platform qualification remain open in [the matrix](./provider-matrix.md).

### Native OpenRouter image SDK subset

Public `OpenRouterImagesProvider` explicitly posts to `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions` with `stream:false` and image modalities, not a conversational fallback. Explicit HTTP(S) versioned/proxy roots or full chat endpoints preserve prefixes/encoded paths; URL credentials/query/fragment refuse. Bearer keys and headers use actual HTTP representation checks and fixed value-free diagnostics. No ambient key, foreign credentials, OAuth/login, model discovery or reserved factory ID is added; chat/stream chat are unsupported.

Direct `generateNativeImages(NativeImageRequest, {signal?})` accepts `{model,input:[{type:'text',text}|{type:'image',mediaType,data}]}` in caller order. Input is descriptor-safe owned JSON (≤12 MiB, 16 parts), with aggregate text ≤32 KiB and the unchanged four-image/8-MiB decoded PNG/JPEG/WebP ceiling. Unpaired text surrogates are removed, valid pairs retained. Known text-only models and unknown configured models refuse image input. Native messages contain one user `content` array of text/data-URL image parts; pinned text-output capability selects `['image','text']` rather than `['image']` modalities. Ordinary configured network retry/deadline/proxy policy applies, including ordinary 504 retries, unlike Decisions' route-local exclusion.

`NativeImageResult` retains API/provider/model, timestamp, optional bounded response ID, first-choice text/images, observed usage and `stopReason:'stop'|'error'|'aborted'`. Both string/object `image_url` data URLs normalize to owned image parts. Empty/malformed/refused/tool-bearing/oversized output clears **all** output with a body-free error; unsupported media and remote image URLs refuse rather than fetching or publishing a partial image batch. Caption-only direct results are allowed, but finite image generation still requires an image. Decoded accounting survives rejected output or observed withdrawal; unparseable/HTTP/held-read failures do not invent usage. Cache read is the reported cached total minus cache writes (floored at zero), with uncached input plus disjoint cache/write/output accounting. Invalid cache counters retain only defensible partial/total counters, not fabricated free usage.

Frozen `OPENROUTER_IMAGE_MODELS` projects all 61 pinned image identities/names/APIs/input/output/token tables and declared resize metadata from installed `data/openrouter.json`: 59 image-input and two text-only profiles. Nineteen have nonzero published token tables; the other 42 zero-only tables are ambiguous because upstream also uses zero for unavailable/invalid/router pricing. Those 42 and unknown IDs omit SDK pricing/calculated cost instead of declaring free images. Public static fields are review data, not service/invoice or complete effective-profile qualification. Declared resize settings (2000×2000, 4.5 MiB, JPEG quality 80) are **not** an implemented resizer/pixel-admission policy; complete media profiles, including vector output, remain open. Only existing supported media/budgets are accepted.

`SpecializedModels`/standalone `runCodemode` use the unchanged finite `{model,prompt,count?,size?}` image contract. This route accepts only count one/omitted and omitted size; unsupported controls refuse before I/O instead of being silently ignored or creating multiple billed calls. Caption text/response IDs remain in the direct native method, not the finite guest result. Invalid/too-many/missing images throw `SpecializedModelError` with decoded accounting, attributed once to the failed nested image/outer result; failed scripts publish no store/images, and unpriced calls keep `costComplete:false`. Hooks, ten-second deadline and all guest JSON/output limits remain unchanged. The direct method uses network policy, not those guest hooks/deadline/journal paths, and does not claim Pi ABI compatibility.

This separate native SDK/standalone guest path does not configure hosts; the explicit mixed SDK/custom-host path above composes A/C/I/T. Account-specific catalogue/OAuth, complete profiles and vendor/platform qualification remain open; see [the matrix](./provider-matrix.md).

### Mixed reasoning/text deltas

An OpenAI-compatible SSE delta containing both `reasoning_content` and `content` publishes and awaits reasoning before text, regardless of JSON property order. Azure/local inherit that normalization. Shared turns likewise await reasoning before text when one extension callback supplies both. Separate callbacks/SSE events retain their input order; HTTP byte chunks do not define a sorting boundary. Final text/reasoning, usage, tool calls, output ceilings and protocol versions are conserved. Independent stateful redactor buffering/flush remains unchanged; this is not global transcript sorting, signed reasoning replay or conformance for every dialect.

### Explicit OpenAI-compatible SSE errors

OpenAI/Azure/local streaming refuses an own truthy top-level `error` before publishing any other fields from that frame or completing pending tool calls. The existing error callback receives only `The provider reported a streaming error.`, never the upstream body/code/message. Absent/null/false markers preserve normal completion, usage and tool calls. This changes neither ordinary chat nor other provider dialects.

Shared turns apply their existing bounded pre-output retry policy; already delivered text or reasoning prevents replay. Defaults remain three attempts in Core/SDK and four in CLI/RPC (2/4/8-second CLI delays), subject to existing host settings. This is error recognition, not a new busy/capacity classifier, backoff policy, strict-tools fallback or retry-budget change. SDK/RPC permit a later explicit prompt after failure.

CLI print/JSON and RPC await persistence of delivered, already-redacted failed text/reasoning before error settlement, with bounded `metadata.outputError` and effective provider/model. Empty/cancelled turns, pending tool calls and invented usage are not saved; ordinary stream failure does not trigger compaction. Print still emits no failed response on stdout; JSON keeps its error/exit framing. Reloaded JSON/RPC message projection recognizes the existing output-error marker without replacing the prefix with diagnostic text. Native and installed tests cover Unicode/UTF-8 fragmentation, redaction, one prior tool effect and explicit reload/resume without duplication/replay; RPC also covers failed reasoning. Redactor-withheld suffixes remain excluded.

The shared loop separately permits one useful compact-and-retry for recognized local/structured HTTP overflow or tool-free length exhaustion, preserving raw failed history and authentic usage. Classification bounds, policy/source/signal checks and exclusions are in [context recovery](./run-lifecycle.md#bounded-useful-context-recovery). The shared generic conversion maps accepted tool images to attributed user attachments after the complete result batch; synthetic attachments never rewrite persisted history. Privately admitted [original Google/Vertex](#googlevertex-tool-result-image-subset), [original OpenAI/Azure R](#original-responses-tool-result-image-subset) and [original Anthropic](#original-anthropic-unsigned-tool-results) use their separately bounded native projections. See [rich results](./run-lifecycle.md#parallel-nested-and-rich-tool-results).

### Final tool-argument JSON

OpenAI-compatible chat/stream, OpenAI Responses SSE/WebSocket, native Bedrock binary streaming and Anthropic streaming share an internal final string decoder. Malformed nonempty JSON refuses the entire batch before any complete `tool_call` event or tool execution, including when an earlier call in that response is valid. The fixed error `The provider tool arguments were invalid JSON.` includes no argument body or parser exception. Shared turns treat this protocol failure as terminal even before text; normal transient-error retries remain unchanged. A started Responses WebSocket failure cannot trigger an SSE replay, and its cached connection is retired.

Empty/absent no-argument strings retain the existing `{}` behavior; valid JSON objects, arrays, primitives and null retain the existing `unknown` input contract. This is not a new object-only or JSON Schema policy, partial-JSON repair, general extension-response/accessor validation or a change to the existing wire/output byte ceilings. Native object-input Gemini/Bedrock-chat/Anthropic-chat paths are not string-decoded. Existing error records, SDK failed-run status and explicit subsequent prompts are reused, not a new protocol version or automatic tool retry.

## Explicit Passive Provider Policies

`collectProviderModelCatalog(providers, { discover: false })` assembles published/configured metadata without invoking discovery capability or model-list callbacks. Existing availability validation and conservative metadata remain; no live-only model or durable cache is invented. The default remains live discovery, preserving Server/Web and embedded-host behavior.

`ResolveProviderCredentialOptions.allowCommands: false` makes a selected command source unavailable before execution **or cached-command consumption**, without falling through to a lower-priority stored key. `allowRefresh: false` leaves OAuth inside the existing 60-second refresh window unavailable and unchanged, without reading refresh settings, calling fetch or publishing credentials. Environment keys, API keys and OAuth outside that window keep their normal precedence. Both options default to the previous behavior; they do not revoke credentials, clear the command cache or prevent an explicit later online resolution. Core never infers these options from a CLI environment variable.

CLI/RPC compose these explicit policies through [offline startup](./cli-reference.md#offline-startup-policy); the environment is captured before trusted extension imports and CLI flags come from the definitive parse. Public Core declarations and actual locally installed consumers exercise the additive booleans. `providers/offline-policy.test.ts` retains encrypted byte identity, real bounded command controls, inert refresh-config getters and positive online refresh/discovery controls. This is startup policy, not a transport firewall or complete offline model catalogue.

## Single model versus plural scope

Core `providers/model-catalog.ts` resolves one model without treating catalogue order as provider authority: exact IDs/names and fuzzy aliases use a sole available configured provider, otherwise shared references remain ambiguous. Alias/date ordering stays provider-local. CLI startup refuses ambiguity before its custom-model/default-provider fallback; explicit qualification and full-ID-first native colon handling remain unchanged.

Non-glob scope entries are intentionally plural: resolve independently within each provider, preserve explicit qualification and deduplicate with first position/thinking pin. Existing glob behavior remains separate. CLI/RPC adapters publish each unresolved scope diagnostic as a fixed stderr warning, including alongside explicit startup `--model`; no new protocol record, persisted setting or public Core API is introduced. See [model selection](./cli-reference.md#model-catalogue-and-selection). Owning Core/CLI ambiguity/startup/process leaves and the genuine installed consumer cover credential states, catalogue permutations, scopes/cycling, trailing commas, native colons and channel purity.

## Numeric reasoning budgets

**Native allocation boundary:** the six original Anthropic numeric profiles use the private simple-allocation policy, not the legacy compatible-transport formula below. Enabled thinking requires at least 1,024 thinking tokens and 1,024 final answer tokens after context clamping, including when a configured zero budget falls back to 1,024. Insufficient space refuses before transport in both facades and private preflight. The [zero-budget reserve correction](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-03-anthropic-zero-reserve.json) binds the 1,024/1,025/2,047/2,048 boundaries using one shared-policy representative; existing six-profile reference tests remain. This is not a vendor tokenizer, media-preparation or whole-MOD-03 certificate.

Core exports readonly `ThinkingBudgets` for `ChatOptions`, `StreamProviderTurnOptions` and `StartAgentRunRequest`, plus the validating `copyThinkingBudgets(unknown)` helper; existing `AgentSettings` budget maps remain mutable and structurally compatible. Its four optional fields are `minimal`, `low`, `medium` and `high`. Settings merge global then trusted-project fields; CLI interactive/print/JSON and RPC read that snapshot at each model call. Interactive `/reload` refreshes it; RPC file changes still require restart. Shared provider-turn admission copies inert own fields before `before:provider`, so hook mutation cannot mutate the source settings. Undefined fields are omitted; inherited fields are ignored. Arrays, unknown keys, accessors and unsafe/negative/fractional/nonfinite values fail with a fixed value-free error. Enabled numeric requests revalidate after hooks.

Legacy compatible Anthropic chat/stream use the selected override, clamped to 1,024–32,768, omit temperature with enabled thinking and preserve the existing `max_tokens >= budget + 1024` expansion. The old invalid 512-token minimal default consequently becomes 1,024. Legacy Gemini/Vertex share one body builder: 2.5-Pro budgets clamp to 128–32,768, 2.5-Flash to 0–24,576, and other model names to 0–8,192. Private original Google/Vertex profiles instead preserve valid explicit overrides down to zero in `google-profiles.ts`, with independent numeric/discrete/default policies. These are provider-owned policies, not complete model capability discovery. Missing overrides retain defaults; direct Core `xhigh`/`max` keep their distinct legacy defaults, while CLI capability clamping may instead select `high`. Absent/off thinking is not enabled by a budget. Effort-only APIs do not consume numeric overrides.

Detached Web runs now compose the same budgets through administrator-scoped configuration, trusted-file fallback and an EN/FR Settings JSON control. Whole scoped maps replace lower maps; `{}` selects provider defaults, `null` restores inheritance, and prompt bodies cannot override the policy. See the [native configuration map](./provider-matrix.md#reasoning-host-composition) and real-browser wire fixture. Child-agent inheritance and ordinary HTTP/compact/WebSocket host-only map composition are also delivered; see the [current MOD-03 requirement review](../../audit/pi/MOD-01-tracking.md#mod-03-current-closure-review) for their separate evidence. This does not implement total-token spending limits or complete model/media profiles. The separately qualified adaptive profile below does not consume numeric overrides. Owning proofs: Core `providers/thinking-budgets.test.ts` captures real loopback HTTP chat/stream payloads and negative admission; CLI `cli/thinking-budgets-process.test.ts` covers emitted print/JSON/RPC hooks and HTTP, trust merge, genuine PTY reload and a strict outside-checkout public-declaration consumer. No live vendor credentials are used.

## Published Anthropic adaptive profile

The exact published `claude-sonnet-4-6` ID now uses one Core policy in both chat and streaming requests. Enabled reasoning sends `thinking: { type: "adaptive" }` and `output_config.effort`, not `budget_tokens`. Capability clamping uses the existing shared metadata: minimal maps to low, low/medium/high remain themselves, max sends native max, and unsupported xhigh clamps upward to max. Explicit off sends `thinking: { type: "disabled" }`; an absent option stays absent. Temperature remains omitted while thinking is enabled.

Numeric `thinkingBudgets` does not apply to this profile. Explicit `ChatOptions.maxTokens` remains exact. Without it, preserve the previous per-requested-level output ceilings: minimal/low 2,048; medium 5,120; high 9,216; xhigh 17,408; max 33,792. CLI/RPC already clamp xhigh to max before the provider call, so that path uses 33,792. This avoids silently reducing enabled adaptive calls to the constructor's 1,024-token default. Off/unspecified calls retain that default. These are AiHarness legacy output limits, not claimed vendor maxima.

Model selection is reevaluated from the actual request model on every call, including configured-model changes and per-call overrides. Only this exact qualified ID selects the new dialect; classic and unqualified/lookalike IDs retain their numeric behavior. Invalid non-undefined thinking values fail before provider HTTP. No new metadata or public API version is invented.

Core `providers/anthropic-thinking.test.ts` captures chat/stream payloads, defaults, overrides, visible reasoning and negative admission. CLI `cli/anthropic-thinking-process.test.ts` runs the actual emitted built-in provider in print/JSON/RPC, plus a five-prompt RPC effort/model/off sequence. Its preload changes only the approved transport origin to a loopback fixture; it does not replace provider construction, metadata, selection or body generation, and refuses other network destinations.

This is a request-dialect qualification, not live vendor certification or full Anthropic support. Other model profiles, adaptive Gemini, complete defaults/capability maps and signed thinking-block preservation across tool continuations remain open. Existing text/reasoning projections are not an opaque provider-transcript round-trip.

## Bounded ordinary request admission

Shared `agent/provider-turn.ts` captures the selected streaming method and redactor bindings before callbacks. Own model-bearing input before extensible JSON redaction and own its result again; reprepare after awaited start observers and on each retry. Mutable hook arrays remain mutable without aliasing the dispatched request. Recognized fields require own data or genuine absence; unrelated legacy getters/serializers are ignored. Keep authentic signal and opaque unused thinking maps outside JSON redaction. Initial malformed hook payloads refuse quietly; later local refusal pairs with the admitted error lifecycle and is not retried unchanged.

Optional `StreamProviderTurnOptions.contextBudget` owns positive safe-integer `contextWindow` and nonnegative `reserveTokens`. Its canonical policy is bounded JSON UTF-8 bytes/4 over projected messages/system/tools,not historical billed usage or vendor tokenization. Reserve the maximum of configured reservation,pre-redaction explicit output and final effective output. Output alone greater than the window refuses before provider work. Core `AgentRuntime` captures an existing known manager policy;private checks revalidate current window/reservation at the guard. Equivalent values/title changes conserve admission,recognized reservation accessors refuse unread and unrelated settings getters remain unused.

Exact first-party `AnthropicProvider` instances and explicit `MiniMaxProvider`/`MiniMaxCnProvider` instances register their own native output preparation;structural/extension providers gain no mandatory capability. MiniMax uses its own selected thinking-added/context-clamped allocation described above,not Anthropic's expansion;its canonical qualification remains held. Capture the configured fallback model before callbacks;prepare used numeric maps after awaited observers without freezing caller maps. For Anthropic,reuse actual `applyAnthropicThinking` and native defaults:1024 without thinking,low budget2048 expands explicit64 to3072,and adaptive high without an explicit maximum produces9216 while explicit64 stays64. Off/adaptive effort-only modes leave unused numeric maps opaque.

[WARNING] Unknown manager windows retain legacy behavior and currently lose later-known ceilings. This is not full effective-model/native-dialect/media admission:unknown media is unmeasured,the current guard only checks independently known non-message overhead in that case,and post-hook model capacity,useful compaction/rebuild/overflow,CLI/RPC budget composition,general lifetime authority and actual-settled accounting remain separate. Do not infer a zero media cost,live-vendor proof or complete compaction from a refusal.

## Critical Flows

### Runtime request

```text
before:provider hook -> own/validate payload -> awaited start observers
  -> re-own redacted payload/native output -> optional canonical budget guard
  -> captured provider.streamChat(messages, callbacks, authentic signal)
  -> awaited, bounded deltas/tool calls
  -> cache-aware normalized usage (uncached input + cache read/write + output)
  -> shared tool loop and message persistence
  -> cost only when provider or published catalogue pricing is authoritative
```

### OpenAI Responses transport

`OpenAiResponsesProvider` supports SSE plus real RFC 6455 `websocket` and `websocket-cached` modes. `auto` attempts WebSocket and falls back to SSE only before the first WebSocket event. Cache identities hash session, endpoint, account and headers; raw session IDs are not keys exposed to diagnostics. Idle sockets are leased exclusively, bounded to 64 entries, expire after five idle minutes or 55 minutes of age, and concurrent requests use an ephemeral socket rather than sharing an active lease.

A completed response without tool calls may retain `previous_response_id` and a context fingerprint. A subsequent comparable request sends only its bounded delta; missing continuation and connection-limit protocol errors retry once. Continuation context is capped at 512 KiB. Bodies, events, queued events, accumulated output and tool calls all have explicit limits, and no SSE fallback occurs after any WebSocket event because that could replay an operation.

### Catalogue refresh

The [05a source-contract review](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-catalogue-contract-review.json) qualifies these existing mechanisms, not a new catalogue framework or service-entitlement certificate:

1. `buildProviderModelCatalog` uses sanitized adapter-owned conversation declarations, including empty, otherwise legacy published entries; an absent configured ID augments the set. Discovery keeps published fields where known. Operator definitions overlay names/caps/windows/output/prices, but the actual adapter still owns API identity/private ceilings.
2. Server ordinary discovery has a five-minute memory TTL, explicit refresh/source-change invalidation, bounded IDs/time/retries and same-source last-success retention on failure. Replacement/removal/rename cannot publish old-source results. Authoritative account discovery is current on each read, without implicit auth retries; empty/logout/failure cannot borrow published/configured/operator availability.
3. Account availability filtering occurs **after** operator overlay. Registry import preserves existing operator rows and snapshots defaults only for new rows; it is not automatic repricing. Replacement during import rejects409. Copilot/Codex and Radius retain their own live grant/config/explicit persisted-cache policies.
4. OpenRouter personalization is explicit SDK/CLI foreground acquisition with key/base/effective-header/generation scope; its snapshot has no implicit Server TTL or remote-revocation polling. Native Server/Settings static393 import/Test409 and catalogue refresh do not call `/models/user`. A trusted explicitly personalized original SDK instance is immediately authoritative in Server; operator rows cannot restore its withdrawn/empty availability. Replacement restores configured static metadata, not another account's snapshot. Adding a new HTTP personalization action/hidden network work is not this contract; pinned model-runtime network refresh also defaults off.
5. Enabled qualified IDs (`provider:model`) remain global/project scoped. Cloud auth-source and finite-operation descriptors remain passive/operation-specific; configured/static available never proves credentials or vendor entitlement. Web receives non-secret metadata and `no-store` HTTP responses.

`packages/server/src/runtime/model-catalog.test.ts` adds only one missing composed OpenRouter static/personalized/operator/scope-empty/replacement proof. Existing Core catalogue, SDK personalization and selected registry import tests supply33 Docker passes (one new), one strict changed root; no product change, new HTTP/UI/network contract, reference SDK/installed/browser/production/full-root campaign. Other05b/cloud/effective-policy/final evidence requirements and historical failures remain separate.

### Installed provider/media package boundary

The [06a package review](../../audit/pi/evidence/MOD-01-package-boundary-review.json) closes the actual changed artifact/declaration risk, not a new functional/model-host campaign. One case in `tests/functional-parity/installed-executable.test.ts` reuses its genuine outside-checkout source rebuild/Core+CLI archives/offline install/no workspace links/executable PATH and install-script-denial safeguards. `fixtures/sdk/mod01-package.ts` compiles against installed strict public declarations (no skipLibCheck), including typed cloud/scoped-tool options and rejected profile-as-key strings. Runtime resolves sharp/saxes only inside consumer, executes the actual packed native worker on independent2400×1200 PNG→2000×1000, parses synthetic web/role XML with separate regions/final-only native credentials/cache/strict tools/two10-token SDK responses, and invokes installed CLI offline named-profile listing without subject disclosure.

Product/package/lock files unchanged. First build/workspace-parity types/one changed strict root/one installed case PASS;399 other harness cases unselected. Original Core wire/emitted print-JSON-RPC/authenticated HTTP/Settings proofs remain independently scoped and are not replaced by import/metadata checks. No reference SDK/account/browser/production/full-root/public registry/OS or all-model certificate; future package/declaration changes recheck affected risks only.06a delivered, other effective/cloud/offline/final audit requirements and historical failures remain.

### Custom lifecycle and restricted rename

Discovery accepts explicit keyless HTTP(S) API bases or supported full request endpoints, preserving proxy paths without synthetic authentication. Server discovery caches bind to the actual provider instance; replacement, reload, rename and old-ID reuse discard stale sources and late results. Explicit disconnection persists across reconciliation/restart. Metadata-only edits omit unchanged secrets; PUT cannot change identity.

`POST /api/provider-registry/custom/:id/rename` requires credentials/configuration administrator capabilities and the ordinary Origin boundary. Its only body fields are `{ id, revision }`; built-in IDs are immutable. `GET /custom` retains its array and exposes passive `X-AiHarness-Provider-Rename` capability metadata. Missing/invalid metadata disables Web rename. Settings names exact custom IDs and reloads canonical providers/auth/catalogue/scoped defaults/effective configuration after every outcome, guarded against retired workspace/session/selection results.

[WARNING] Rename is OFF by default. Only `AI_HARNESS_PROVIDER_RENAME_EXCLUSIVE_HOST=1` (exact value) or `providerRenameExclusiveHost: true` opts into a temporary restricted SINGLE-WRITER host. Stop all CLI/other servers/external writers first; independently administer data/session/config/trust/installed-code paths outside editable allowed roots. Activating over a still-owned prior host is refused. This is not OS process isolation or shared-runtime admission.

Actual awaited operation leases cover session/config/profile/start/reload owners, including disconnects and queued child admission. Detached work continuing after HTTP 202 is separately checked through fresh active-run inventories. Rename owns exclusion through registry rollback/reconciliation and the final intent-settlement handshake. Busy agents/PTYs, current or historical session references, qualified disabled child profiles, environment selectors, global/project agent settings and unsafe/legacy/oversized inventories refuse rather than rewrite history or grant trust.

Restricted mode refuses legacy chat/upgrades, shell/PTY, executable package/extension commands, active MCP, file/workspace HTTP (including Git helpers), and unleased model/tool/resource mutations. It imports no third-party Node generation, injects no persistent MCP host into agents, refuses credential commands and cancels bash/PowerShell/Git-search/codemode tools. Default-OFF ordinary host/npm behavior is unchanged. Privileged embedders remain responsible for their own code and manual listeners.

The bounded fresh ConfigurationStore transaction rewrites only exact provider and `oldId:` model/enabled-model references across global/projects/sessions, refuses non-canonical case/whitespace or slash-qualified aliases before publishing an intent, preserves inheritance/siblings and compares publication bytes plus observed file identity for rollback. Registry CAS likewise detects observable replacement/ABA. These are optimistic checks, not hostile-writer atomic compare-exchange.

[WARNING] Two metadata files are NOT crash-atomic. A private bounded `config.json.provider-intent.json` pending/malformed/linked/uncertain intent blocks initialization, listener publication and mutations. Never auto-replay, clear by PID, or force-clear online. With EVERY writer stopped, preserve metadata/credentials/intent/reference inventories, inspect intervening edits and choose/verify one coherent outcome without guessing or moving secrets or rewriting history. Remove the intent only after explicit offline reconciliation, then restart. A settled marker is safe to retain.

Provider OAuth remains Device Flow; it has no pasted callback endpoint. The shared inline `OAuthPasteInput` is used by genuine MCP callback flows. It is inert before explicit flow admission, bounded to 8,192 characters, transient, non-autofocusing and acknowledgement-gated; Enter preserves native composition and legacy keyCode 229 rather than submitting. Original MCP labels/layout/polling/trust/status/cancel ownership remain in the host.

### Add a provider

1. Add the Core implementation and factory mapping.
2. Add Server configuration, credentials, discovery/OAuth, and proxy handling as applicable.
3. Update CLI command/RPC initialization and Web settings/catalogue behavior.
4. Export public Core contracts from `packages/core/src/index.ts`.
5. Test streaming completion, cancellation, malformed remote data, secrets, and usage.

## Common Changes

### Change model metadata

Update the owning adapter's reviewed profile/declaration or the legacy `packages/core/src/providers/model-metadata.ts` table as appropriate. Keep `provider-model-metadata.ts` admission/copy rules shared; verify offline assembly, Server runtime/import/operator overrides, CLI RPC descriptors, activation, capability filtering, prices, API serialization and actual Settings selectors.

### Change streaming

Update the Core provider and shared tool loop expectations. Preserve incremental chunks, one final assembly, AbortSignal propagation, reasoning/tool events, and no double completion. Await every chunk callback; consumer delivery errors are terminal and must not be retried, while a non-cooperative extension producer must fail through the bounded queue guard.

### Change provider administration

Update registry/service/routes, `packages/web/src/services/api.ts`, Settings UI, security capability tests, and secret-redaction coverage together.
